And find is there any way i can control how much header I want in/out of the pdf its reading and converting. Basically, the PDFs i m using have large headers, and marker-pdf is mostly including those header texts even though i havent enabled the headers

Worked for 1m 27s
Yes. The newest Marker code lets you control how much of the top of each page is treated as removable header material. The most relevant setting is header_zone.

The confusing part is that Marker has two separate header-removal mechanisms.

Why your headers are still appearing
keep_pageheader_in_output=False—the default—does not mean “remove all text near the top of the page.”

It only excludes blocks that the layout model successfully classified as PageHeader. The PageHeader block has ignore_for_output=True by default.

If the layout model classifies your large header as:

Text

SectionHeader

Caption

then the normal page-header output setting cannot remove it. This explains why header text can still appear even though you never enabled --keep_pageheader_in_output.

Also, PageHeaderProcessor only moves already-detected PageHeader blocks to the beginning of the page structure. It does not decide which content is a header.

This problem has repeatedly appeared in the repository:

Issue #193: Restrict areas of a page that are read

Issue #433: Remove headers and footers

Issue #557: Unnecessary header remains in Markdown

Issue #1012: Header pictures still retained

The current solution: MarginaliaProcessor
On July 4, 2026, Marker added MarginaliaProcessor specifically to remove running headers and footers that the layout model misclassified.

It is included in the default PDF pipeline in PdfConverter.

Its relevant defaults are:

header_zone = 0.08
footer_zone = 0.13
max_height_frac = 0.035
max_chars = 150
These values are fractions of the page:

header_zone=0.08: only the top 8% is considered header territory.

footer_zone=0.13: only the bottom 13% is considered footer territory.

max_height_frac=0.035: a removable header block may occupy at most 3.5% of page height.

max_chars=150: the candidate block may contain at most 150 characters.

For your PDFs, the default 8% header zone and 3.5% maximum block height are probably too small.

Recommended configuration
Start with:

{
  "header_zone": 0.15,
  "max_height_frac": 0.08,
  "max_chars": 300,
  "keep_pageheader_in_output": false
}
This means:

Consider the top 15% of each page as possible header territory.

Allow an individual header text block to be up to 8% of page height.

Allow longer header text.

Continue excluding blocks explicitly classified as PageHeader.

CLI
On a current Marker installation:

marker_single input.pdf \
  --header_zone 0.15 \
  --max_height_frac 0.08 \
  --max_chars 300
You do not need to pass --keep_pageheader_in_output; its default is already False.

For a reusable configuration file, create marker_config.json:

{
  "header_zone": 0.15,
  "max_height_frac": 0.08,
  "max_chars": 300,
  "keep_pageheader_in_output": false,
  "keep_pagefooter_in_output": false
}
Then run:

marker_single input.pdf --config_json marker_config.json
Marker dynamically exposes processor attributes as CLI/configuration options through its CustomClickPrinter.

Python API
from marker.config.parser import ConfigParser
from marker.converters.pdf import PdfConverter
from marker.models import create_model_dict

config = {
    "output_format": "markdown",

    # Exclude blocks Marker correctly identifies as headers/footers
    "keep_pageheader_in_output": False,
    "keep_pagefooter_in_output": False,

    # Catch large headers misclassified as normal text
    "header_zone": 0.15,
    "footer_zone": 0.13,
    "max_height_frac": 0.08,
    "max_chars": 300,
}

config_parser = ConfigParser(config)

converter = PdfConverter(
    artifact_dict=create_model_dict(),
    config=config_parser.generate_config_dict(),
    processor_list=config_parser.get_processors(),
    renderer=config_parser.get_renderer(),
)

rendered = converter("input.pdf")
How to select the right values
Suppose your PDF page is 1,000 pixels or points high:

Setting	Effective region
header_zone=0.08	Top 80 units
header_zone=0.12	Top 120 units
header_zone=0.15	Top 150 units
header_zone=0.20	Top 200 units
Marker checks the bottom edge of the header block. Therefore, if a header begins at the top but extends down to 14% of the page, header_zone must be at least approximately 0.14.

A practical progression is:

0.10 → 0.12 → 0.15 → 0.18
Avoid jumping immediately to something like 0.25, because genuine titles and first paragraphs near the top could be removed.

For large multi-line headers, increase both:

{
  "header_zone": 0.15,
  "max_height_frac": 0.08
}
Increasing only header_zone may not work: the candidate can still be rejected for being taller than the default max_height_frac=0.035.

Important limitations
The positional processor does not indiscriminately delete the top portion of the page. A block must satisfy all these conditions:

It must be entirely inside the configured header zone.

It must appear above the detected body content.

Its height must be below max_height_frac.

Its text must be shorter than max_chars.

Its type must be Text, SectionHeader, or Caption.

This protects real titles and top-of-page content, but it also means the setting is not a hard crop.

It will not remove:

Header logos or pictures.

Headers detected as tables, figures, or other non-text block types.

Very tall header blocks unless max_height_frac is increased.

A header joined into the first body paragraph.

Text whose bounding box crosses below header_zone.

Header images are currently a known problem, reported in issue #1012.

Version warning
MarginaliaProcessor was introduced in commit a86bee6, dated July 4, 2026. Support for respecting the keep-header/footer settings was refined in commit fc3c3ba, dated July 7, 2026.

Check your installed version and whether the processor exists:

pip show marker-pdf
python -c "from marker.processors.marginalia import MarginaliaProcessor; print(MarginaliaProcessor.header_zone)"
If the import fails, your installed Marker version is too old for this solution. Upgrade:

pip install --upgrade marker-pdf
Or install the current repository version:

pip install --upgrade git+https://github.com/datalab-to/marker.git
My recommended starting command for your large-header PDFs is:

marker_single input.pdf \
  --header_zone 0.15 \
  --max_height_frac 0.08 \
  --max_chars 300 \
  --debug
Use the debug output to inspect the detected bounding boxes, then adjust header_zone based on where the unwanted header’s bottom edge lies.


Yes. You can use a loop that invokes marker_single repeatedly, processing 20 pages per invocation.

Marker’s --page_range is zero-indexed, so the batches are:

0-19 → visible pages 1–20

20-39 → visible pages 21–40

40-59 → visible pages 41–60

PowerShell
For a 100-page document:

for ($start = 0; $start -lt 100; $start += 20) {
    $end = [Math]::Min($start + 19, 99)
    $batch = [int]($start / 20) + 1

    marker_single "input.pdf" `
        --mode balanced `
        --page_range "$start-$end" `
        --output_dir "output\batch_$batch"
}
This creates:

output/
├── batch_1/   # Pages 1–20
├── batch_2/   # Pages 21–40
├── batch_3/   # Pages 41–60
├── batch_4/   # Pages 61–80
└── batch_5/   # Pages 81–100
Separate output directories are important because every conversion uses the same input filename and could otherwise overwrite the previous output.

PowerShell one-liner
for ($s=0; $s -lt 100; $s+=20) { $e=[Math]::Min($s+19,99); $b=[int]($s/20)+1; marker_single "input.pdf" --mode balanced --page_range "$s-$e" --output_dir "output\batch_$b" }
Automatically detect the page count
If pdfinfo is installed:

$pages = [int]((pdfinfo "input.pdf" | Select-String "^Pages:").ToString().Split(":")[1].Trim())

for ($start = 0; $start -lt $pages; $start += 20) {
    $end = [Math]::Min($start + 19, $pages - 1)
    $batch = [int]($start / 20) + 1

    marker_single "input.pdf" `
        --mode balanced `
        --page_range "$start-$end" `
        --output_dir "output\batch_$batch"
}
Bash/Linux
pages=$(pdfinfo "input.pdf" | awk '/^Pages:/ {print $2}')

for ((start=0, batch=1; start<pages; start+=20, batch++)); do
    end=$((start + 19))
    ((end >= pages)) && end=$((pages - 1))

    marker_single "input.pdf" \
        --mode balanced \
        --page_range "$start-$end" \
        --output_dir "output/batch_$batch"
done
If processing batches sequentially, keep the Surya inference server loaded:

$env:SURYA_INFERENCE_KEEP_ALIVE = "1"
Then run the loop. This avoids reloading the balanced-mode model for every 20-page batch. Passing something such as --page_range "0-19,20-39,40-59" would not create separate batches—it would simply process all those pages in one conversion.


Is there a way that i can send a document's pages in batches of 20 in a single command, while ll batches running parallely?

Yes. In PowerShell 7, ForEach-Object -Parallel can launch all 20-page batches concurrently from one command.

$inputPdf = "input.pdf"
$outputRoot = "output"
$batchSize = 20
$pages = [int]((pdfinfo $inputPdf | Select-String "^Pages:").ToString().Split(":")[1].Trim())

$starts = 0..([Math]::Ceiling($pages / $batchSize) - 1) |
    ForEach-Object { $_ * $batchSize }

$starts | ForEach-Object -Parallel {
    $start = $_
    $end = [Math]::Min($start + $using:batchSize - 1, $using:pages - 1)
    $batchNumber = [int]($start / $using:batchSize) + 1
    $outputDir = Join-Path $using:outputRoot "batch_$('{0:D3}' -f $batchNumber)"

    marker_single $using:inputPdf `
        --mode balanced `
        --page_range "$start-$end" `
        --output_dir $outputDir
} -ThrottleLimit 3
This automatically creates batches such as:

batch_001 → pages 1–20
batch_002 → pages 21–40
batch_003 → pages 41–60
...
-ThrottleLimit 3 means at most three batches run simultaneously. It does not limit the total number of batches.

Recommended approach for balanced mode:

$env:SURYA_INFERENCE_KEEP_ALIVE = "1"
$env:SURYA_INFERENCE_PARALLEL = "8"
However, there is an important distinction:

SURYA_INFERENCE_PARALLEL parallelizes page-level inference through one Surya server.

ForEach-Object -Parallel launches multiple complete Marker processes.

Running every batch simultaneously is usually a bad idea on one GPU. Each process also performs PDF parsing, layout processing, OCR preparation, and rendering, and may attempt to initialize its own models or server. This can cause:

GPU out-of-memory errors

Multiple copies of models in VRAM

Heavy RAM consumption

vLLM request congestion

Lower overall throughput

Random batch failures

Start with:

-ThrottleLimit 2
Then test 3 or 4. If you have one GPU, I would generally prefer:

2–3 Marker processes
+
one shared inference server
+
SURYA_INFERENCE_PARALLEL tuned to server capacity
If you already know the document contains exactly 100 pages, the shorter version is:

0,20,40,60,80 | ForEach-Object -Parallel {
    $start = $_
    $end = $start + 19
    $batch = [int]($start / 20) + 1

    marker_single "input.pdf" `
        --mode balanced `
        --page_range "$start-$end" `
        --output_dir "output\batch_$batch"
} -ThrottleLimit 3
Remember that Marker page ranges are zero-indexed, and batch outputs will be separate. You will need a final step to combine the generated Markdown/JSON/HTML files in numerical batch order.

Worked for 18s
Your current code processes each entire document sequentially in fast mode. To process each PDF in parallel 20-page batches, change it to:

Use balanced mode.

Determine each PDF’s page count.

Generate ranges such as 0–19, 20–39, etc.

Run a separate PdfConverter inside each worker.

Merge the Markdown results in page order.

Limit concurrency—start with two workers.

Add this import:

from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed
from pypdf import PdfReader
Set the Surya options before importing Marker:

os.environ.setdefault("SURYA_INFERENCE_KEEP_ALIVE", "1")
os.environ.setdefault("SURYA_INFERENCE_PARALLEL", "8")
Then add:

BATCH_SIZE = 20
MAX_PARALLEL_BATCHES = 2
Create a batch-conversion function:

def convert_batch(
    source_path: Path,
    start_page: int,
    end_page: int,
) -> tuple[int, str]:
    # Each worker gets its own converter. Do not share one PdfConverter
    # instance between concurrent workers.
    converter = PdfConverter(
        artifact_dict=create_model_dict(),
        config={
            "mode": "balanced",
            "page_range": list(range(start_page, end_page + 1)),

            # Optional large-header removal settings:
            "keep_pageheader_in_output": False,
            "keep_pagefooter_in_output": False,
            "header_zone": 0.15,
            "max_height_frac": 0.08,
            "max_chars": 300,
        },
    )

    rendered = converter(str(source_path))
    markdown, _, _ = text_from_rendered(rendered)

    return start_page, markdown
Replace your existing converter = PdfConverter(...) and conversion loop with:

converted = 0
failures: list[tuple[Path, Exception]] = []

for source_path in source_files:
    output_path = output_path_for(source_path)
    output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    print(f"Converting: {source_path.relative_to(PROJECT_ROOT)}")

    try:
        if source_path.suffix.lower() != ".pdf":
            raise ValueError("Parallel page batching currently supports PDF files only.")

        page_count = len(PdfReader(str(source_path)).pages)

        batches = [
            (start, min(start + BATCH_SIZE - 1, page_count - 1))
            for start in range(0, page_count, BATCH_SIZE)
        ]

        print(
            f"Processing {page_count} pages in "
            f"{len(batches)} batch(es), with up to "
            f"{MAX_PARALLEL_BATCHES} running concurrently."
        )

        batch_results: dict[int, str] = {}

        with ThreadPoolExecutor(
            max_workers=MAX_PARALLEL_BATCHES
        ) as executor:
            futures = {
                executor.submit(
                    convert_batch,
                    source_path,
                    start_page,
                    end_page,
                ): (start_page, end_page)
                for start_page, end_page in batches
            }

            for future in as_completed(futures):
                start_page, end_page = futures[future]
                _, markdown = future.result()
                batch_results[start_page] = markdown

                print(
                    f"Completed visible pages "
                    f"{start_page + 1}-{end_page + 1}"
                )

        # Preserve the original page order when combining results.
        markdown_output = "\n\n".join(
            batch_results[start_page].rstrip()
            for start_page, _ in batches
        )

        output_path.write_text(markdown_output + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
        converted += 1

        print(f"Markdown written to: {output_path.relative_to(PROJECT_ROOT)}")

    except Exception as error:
        failures.append((source_path, error))
        print(f"FAILED: {source_path.name}: {error}")
Install the page-count dependency if necessary:

uv add pypdf
Then your existing single command remains:

uv run python marker_test.py
Important caution: your script forces the llamacpp inference backend. Two simultaneous converters may contend for the same inference server or attempt overlapping initialization. Start with:

MAX_PARALLEL_BATCHES = 2
If you encounter server startup conflicts, out-of-memory errors, or slower performance, use one document-level worker and let:

SURYA_INFERENCE_PARALLEL=8
provide inference-level parallelism instead. Also, parallel 20-page batches do not inherently improve recognition quality; they mainly change scheduling and memory behavior.

--- pagefooter/pageheader ---
from marker.schema import BlockTypes
from marker.schema.blocks import Block


class PageHeader(Block):
    block_type: BlockTypes = BlockTypes.PageHeader
    block_description: str = (
        "Text that appears at the top of a page, like a page title."
    )
    replace_output_newlines: bool = True
    ignore_for_output: bool = True
    html: str | None = None

    def assemble_html(self, document, child_blocks, parent_structure, block_config):
        if block_config and block_config.get("keep_pageheader_in_output"):
            self.ignore_for_output = False

        if self.html and not self.ignore_for_output:
            return self.html

        return super().assemble_html(
            document, child_blocks, parent_structure, block_config
        )

OPENING_TAG_REGEX = re.compile(r"<((?:math|i|b))(?:\s+[^>]*)?>")
CLOSING_TAG_REGEX = re.compile(r"</((?:math|i|b))>")
TAG_MAPPING = {
    'i': 'italic',
    'b': 'bold',
    'math': 'math',
    'mark': 'highlight',
    'sub': 'subscript',
    'sup': 'superscript',
    'small': 'small',
    'u': 'underline',
    'code': 'code'

def strings_to_classes(items: List[str]) -> List[type]:
    classes = []
    for item in items:
        module_name, class_name = item.rsplit('.', 1)
        module = import_module(module_name)
        classes.append(getattr(module, class_name))
    return classes


def classes_to_strings(items: List[type]) -> List[str]:
    for item in items:
        if not inspect.isclass(item):
            raise ValueError(f"Item {item} is not a class")

    return [f"{item.__module__}.{item.__name__}" for item in items]


def verify_config_keys(obj):
    annotations = inspect.get_annotations(obj.__class__)

    none_vals = ""
    for attr_name, annotation in annotations.items():
        if isinstance(annotation, type(Annotated[str, ""])):
            value = getattr(obj, attr_name)
            if value is None:
                none_vals += f"{attr_name}, "

    assert len(none_vals) == 0, f"In order to use {obj.__class__.__name__}, you must set the configuration values `{none_vals}`."

    cls_name = cls.__class__.__name__
    if config is None:
=== BASE CONVERTER ===
import inspect
from typing import Optional, List, Type

from pydantic import BaseModel

from marker.processors import BaseProcessor
from marker.processors.llm import BaseLLMSimpleBlockProcessor
from marker.processors.llm.llm_meta import LLMSimpleBlockMetaProcessor
from marker.util import assign_config, download_font


class BaseConverter:
    def __init__(self, config: Optional[BaseModel | dict] = None):
        assign_config(self, config)
        self.config = config
        self.llm_service = None

        # Download render font, needed for some providers
        download_font()

    def __call__(self, *args, **kwargs):
        raise NotImplementedError

    def resolve_dependencies(self, cls):
        init_signature = inspect.signature(cls.__init__)
        parameters = init_signature.parameters

        resolved_kwargs = {}
        for param_name, param in parameters.items():
            if param_name == 'self':
                continue
            elif param_name == 'config':
                resolved_kwargs[param_name] = self.config
            elif param.name in self.artifact_dict:
                resolved_kwargs[param_name] = self.artifact_dict[param_name]
            elif param.default != inspect.Parameter.empty:
                resolved_kwargs[param_name] = param.default
            else:
                raise ValueError(f"Cannot resolve dependency for parameter: {param_name}")

        return cls(**resolved_kwargs)

    def initialize_processors(self, processor_cls_lst: List[Type[BaseProcessor]]) -> List[BaseProcessor]:
        processors = []
        for processor_cls in processor_cls_lst:
            processors.append(self.resolve_dependencies(processor_cls))

        simple_llm_processors = [p for p in processors if issubclass(type(p), BaseLLMSimpleBlockProcessor)]
        other_processors = [p for p in processors if not issubclass(type(p), BaseLLMSimpleBlockProcessor)]

        if not simple_llm_processors:
            return processors

        llm_positions = [i for i, p in enumerate(processors) if issubclass(type(p), BaseLLMSimpleBlockProcessor)]
        insert_position = max(0, llm_positions[-1] - len(simple_llm_processors) + 1)

        meta_processor = LLMSimpleBlockMetaProcessor(
            processor_lst=simple_llm_processors,
            llm_service=self.llm_service,
            config=self.config,
        )
        other_processors.insert(insert_position, meta_processor)
        return other_processors=== keep_pageheader grep ===

def assign_config(cls, config: BaseModel | dict | None):
    cls_name = cls.__class__.__name__
    if config is None:
        return
    elif isinstance(config, BaseModel):
        dict_config = config.dict()
    elif isinstance(config, dict):
        dict_config = config
    else:
        raise ValueError("config must be a dict or a pydantic BaseModel")

    for k in dict_config:
        if hasattr(cls, k):
            setattr(cls, k, dict_config[k])
    for k in dict_config:
        if cls_name not in k:
            continue
        # Enables using class-specific keys, like "MarkdownRenderer_remove_blocks"
        split_k = k.removeprefix(cls_name + "_")

        if hasattr(cls, split_k):
            setattr(cls, split_k, dict_config[k])


def parse_range_str(range_str: str) -> List[int]:
    range_lst = range_str.split(",")
    page_lst = []
    for i in range_lst:
        if "-" in i:
            start, end = i.split("-")
            page_lst += list(range(int(start), int(end) + 1))
        else:
            page_lst.append(int(i))
    page_lst = sorted(list(set(page_lst)))  # Deduplicate page numbers and sort in order
    return page_lst


def matrix_intersection_area(boxes1: List[List[float]], boxes2: List[List[float]]) -> np.ndarray:
    if len(boxes1) == 0 or len(boxes2) == 0:
        return np.zeros((len(boxes1), len(boxes2)))

    boxes1 = np.array(boxes1)
    boxes2 = np.array(boxes2)

Verified against your actual install. Here's the situation:

The claim doesn't hold for your version
There is no MarginaliaProcessor in marker-pdf==1.10.2 — the processors directory has 20 modules and no marginalia.py, and pdf.py:74-103 lists the default pipeline with no such entry. The July 4, 2026 date is past my knowledge cutoff so I can't confirm whether it landed upstream later, but it is definitively not available to you unless you upgrade — and upgrading is risky here given the Pillow pinning that forced the three-venv split in the first place.

Also, those defaults (header_zone=0.08, max_chars=150) don't match any config key present in the package. Treat that answer as unverified.

Why your headers leak (the premise is slightly off)
You said you "haven't enabled headers" — correct, and that's already working. PageHeader sets ignore_for_output: bool = True, and the renderer's keep_pageheader_in_output defaults to False (renderers/init.py:26-33).

So anything reaching your Markdown was never labeled PageHeader. The layout model classified it as Text or SectionHeader. No amount of header-suppression config will touch it — you need to catch it by geometry or repetition instead.

What you can actually do today
PdfConverter(artifact_dict=..., config={...}) flows a flat dict into every processor and the renderer via assign_config, which also supports ClassName_key prefixing.

Tier 1 — tune the repetition detector. IgnoreTextProcessor already targets exactly this, fuzzy-matching text repeated across pages:


converter = PdfConverter(
    artifact_dict=create_model_dict(),
    config={
        "IgnoreTextProcessor_common_element_threshold": 0.1,  # default 0.2
        "IgnoreTextProcessor_common_element_min_blocks": 2,   # default 3
        "IgnoreTextProcessor_text_match_threshold": 80,       # default 90
    },
)
Real limitation: ignoretext.py:50-60 only inspects the first and last structured block per page. A "large header" that spans two or three blocks will only ever have its first block considered — the rest survive regardless of threshold. If your headers are multi-line/multi-block, this tier will not be enough on its own.

Tier 2 — geometric suppression (the real fix). This is what the described MarginaliaProcessor does, and it's ~25 lines:


from marker.processors import BaseProcessor
from marker.schema import BlockTypes

class MarginaliaProcessor(BaseProcessor):
    block_types = (BlockTypes.Text, BlockTypes.SectionHeader, BlockTypes.TextInlineMath)
    header_zone = 0.08       # top 8% of page
    footer_zone = 0.13       # bottom 13%
    max_height_frac = 0.035  # ignore tall blocks — real content, not marginalia
    max_chars = 150

    def __call__(self, document):
        for page in document.pages:
            page_top = page.polygon.y_start
            page_h = page.polygon.height
            if page_h <= 0:
                continue
            for block in page.contained_blocks(document, self.block_types):
                rel_top = (block.polygon.y_start - page_top) / page_h
                rel_bot = (block.polygon.y_end - page_top) / page_h
                if block.polygon.height / page_h > self.max_height_frac:
                    continue
                if len(block.raw_text(document)) > self.max_chars:
                    continue
                if rel_bot < self.header_zone or rel_top > (1 - self.footer_zone):
                    block.ignore_for_output = True
Wire it in by subclassing rather than passing processor_list — that param goes through strings_to_classes and needs importable dotted paths, whereas default_processors takes classes directly:


class MarginaliaPdfConverter(PdfConverter):
    default_processors = tuple(
        p for pair in (
            (proc, MarginaliaProcessor) if proc is IgnoreTextProcessor else (proc,)
            for proc in PdfConverter.default_processors
        ) for p in pair
    )
The zone fractions become tunable via config for free — assign_config picks up any attribute name that exists on the class, so {"MarginaliaProcessor_header_zone": 0.12} works without extra code.

Tier 3 — relabeling. BlockRelabelProcessor accepts {"block_relabel_str": "SectionHeader:PageHeader:0.6"}. Note the logic at block_relabel.py:70-72 is inverted from what you'd guess — it relabels when confidence is below the threshold. It's also document-wide with no positional awareness, so it will demote genuine mid-page section headers the model was unsure about. I'd skip it.

Recommendation
Start with Tier 1 since it's a config-only change you can test in one run. If the headers are multi-block, go to Tier 2 — it's the only approach that reliably handles them, and it's independent of any upstream release.

One caution specific to this repo: this changes what lands in marker_results/, which is the input to chunking/ingest.py. Chunk boundaries and the chunk_seq numbering will shift, so any comparison against an existing chunk-runs/ report becomes apples-to-oranges.

Read C:\Users\LOQ\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\c--Users-LOQ-Desktop-Projects-Document-Loaders\7739be4c-9b2b-4127-bc1c-ca5498c51a64\tasks\b91zgosy4.output
Good news on the original question, plus a significant catch. The torch download (1.8 GB) is still running; I'll confirm CUDA when it lands.

1. MarginaliaProcessor — the claim checks out
Everything in what you read is accurate for 2.0.0. marginalia.py exists, is registered in the default pipeline at pdf.py:94, and the four defaults match exactly. My earlier answer was right for 1.10.2 and is now obsolete — you can throw away the custom processor I sketched, it's upstream now and better guarded than mine.

It's stricter than my version in a way that matters: a block must also be the extremal text block on the page (y1 <= body_top), so an edge-adjacent body paragraph won't get eaten. Tuning is plain config:


converter = PdfConverter(
    artifact_dict=create_model_dict(),
    config={"MarginaliaProcessor_header_zone": 0.12},  # default 0.08
)
If headers still leak after the GPU fix, raise header_zone first, then max_chars — but re-check after, because of the next item.

2. Your torch needed cu130, not cu128
The RTX 5050 is Blackwell (sm_120), so CUDA 12.8+ is mandatory. But the cu128 index tops out at torch 2.9.1 — it has no 2.13.0. Only the cu130 index carries 2.13.0+cu130, matching your installed version exactly. So this is a pure CPU→CUDA swap with no version drift, which is why marker/surya constraints stay satisfied.

The reason uv did this to you: on Windows, PyPI's default torch wheels are CPU-only. Any install that resolves torch without --index-url silently lands you on CPU. Pin the index going forward.

Unrelated leftover: torchaudio 2.11.0+cu128 is still installed, built against torch 2.11 ABI. Nothing in site-packages imports it, so it's inert — but I left it alone rather than removing something you didn't ask about.

3. The catch — CPU torch silently changed your conversion pipeline
marker 2.0.0 added a mode setting, and pdf.py:130-133 picks the default from your device:


config["mode"] = "balanced" if settings.TORCH_DEVICE_MODEL == "cuda" else "fast"
balanced = VLM layout model + full-page OCR. fast = rf-detr/onnx layout + tables, block-OCR only on garbled content. Two genuinely different extraction pipelines.

So while torch was CPU, marker was running fast — not just slower, but a different converter. Once CUDA is live it flips to balanced with no code change on your side. Anything in marker_results/ is now three-way incomparable: 1.10.2 output, 2.0.0-fast output, and 2.0.0-balanced output. Given CLAUDE.md frames this repo as a comparison harness, I'd regenerate the whole corpus once CUDA is confirmed rather than trusting a mixed tree.

You can also pin {"mode": "balanced"} explicitly to make runs device-independent and stop this from silently recurring.

4. Also changed in 2.0.0
OrderProcessor was dropped from the default pipeline (it was first in 1.10.2). Worth knowing given the reading-order and content-reordering defects already logged in Marker-PDF Report.md — that's a plausible mover on those symptoms, in either direction.

CLAUDE.md still documents marker-pdf==1.10.2 and the docs describe the 1.10.2 pipeline.

Once the download finishes I'll verify torch.cuda.is_available() and that the converter resolves to balanced. Want me to then update CLAUDE.md to 2.0.0 + the cu130 index command, and log the mode-default trap in docs/Bugs.md? That one is exactly the kind of silent-behavior-change that file exists for.

    SURYA_GGUF_LOCAL_MMPROJ_PATH: Optional[str] = None

    # Backend selection
    SURYA_INFERENCE_BACKEND: Optional[str] = None  # "vllm" | "llamacpp" | None (auto)
    SURYA_INFERENCE_URL: Optional[str] = None  # external server, skip spawn
    SURYA_INFERENCE_AUTOSTART: bool = True
    # Leave an auto-spawned server running after the process exits so later
    # commands attach to it instead of re-spawning (avoids repeated startup /
    # model-load cost). Stop it manually when done — see `surya/inference`.
    SURYA_INFERENCE_KEEP_ALIVE: bool = False
    SURYA_INFERENCE_HOST: str = "127.0.0.1"
    SURYA_INFERENCE_PORT: Optional[int] = None  # None = pick a free port
    # Client-side concurrent request count. None = let the backend pick a
    # sensible default (vllm scales to the server's max_num_seqs / GPU
    # capacity; llama.cpp uses a conservative slot count). Set an int to
    # override.
    SURYA_INFERENCE_PARALLEL: Optional[int] = None
    # Per-parallel-slot KV-cache budget for the llama.cpp backend. Worst-case
    # one OCR request: ~2k for image prefill + SURYA_MAX_TOKENS_FULL_PAGE
    # (8192) generation + ~2k prompt/chat-template overhead ≈ 12k. Below this
    # llama-server silently truncates outputs once a slot fills.
    SURYA_INFERENCE_CTX_PER_SLOT: int = 12288
    # Optional override for the *total* ctx passed to llama-server. When None
    # (default), total = max(16384, PARALLEL * CTX_PER_SLOT). Set this only
    # if you've hand-tuned for a specific machine.
    SURYA_INFERENCE_CTX_SIZE: Optional[int] = None
    SURYA_INFERENCE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS: float = 600.0
    SURYA_INFERENCE_STARTUP_TIMEOUT: float = 600.0
    SURYA_INFERENCE_LOGPROBS: bool = True
    # Force layout/table_rec output through a JSON schema via guided decoding.
    # Eliminates malformed-JSON failures at small decode-throughput cost.
    SURYA_GUIDED_LAYOUT: bool = True
    # Disabled: with no minItems in TABLE_REC_JSON_SCHEMA, the constrained
    # decoder closes the array after one element at temperature=0. The model
    # produces well-formed JSON without the schema.
    SURYA_GUIDED_TABLE_REC: bool = False

    # Token budgets
    SURYA_MAX_TOKENS_LAYOUT: int = 3072
    SURYA_MAX_TOKENS_TABLE_REC: int = 3072
    SURYA_MAX_TOKENS_BLOCK_CEILING: int = 8192
    SURYA_MAX_TOKENS_FULL_PAGE: int = 12288

    # Full-page OCR: progressive-temperature regeneration before block-mode fallback.
    # Off by default (single greedy pass -> block fallback); opt-in for benchmarking.
    SURYA_FULLPAGE_REGEN: bool = False

    BBOX_SCALE: int = 1000

    # vllm

Docker isn't the real story here — marker 2.0.0 changed architecture. Confirmed state:


llama-server   not installed
docker CLI     present, daemon not running
VRAM           8151 MiB (8 GB)
Root cause
surya-ocr 0.22.1 no longer runs the VLM in-process. It runs surya-ocr-2 in a separate inference server speaking OpenAI-compatible chat completions, and auto-picks the backend at inference/init.py:62-68:


if _has_nvidia_gpu():
    return "vllm"
return "llamacpp"
The vllm backend spawns Docker image vllm/vllm-openai:v0.20.1 (settings.py:96). Daemon's down, so every file fails.

This is partly my doing, and I called it wrong earlier. I said restoring CUDA would just switch you to a higher-quality pipeline. But mode="balanced" routes layout itself through the VLM (layout.py:88-90), making the server mandatory. On CPU torch you were in fast mode, which uses the local rf-detr/onnx predictor and only touches the VLM for garbled blocks — so you likely never spawned a server. My fix surfaced this.

Note _has_nvidia_gpu() falls back to nvidia-smi, so it would have selected vllm even on CPU torch. Reverting torch would not have avoided this.

Options
1. SURYA_INFERENCE_BACKEND=llamacpp — spawns llama-server natively, no Docker, quantized GGUF from datalab-to/surya-ocr-2-gguf. Needs the llama-server binary installed (not present). Best fit for an 8 GB laptop. My recommendation.

2. Start Docker Desktop — one command, but: ~10 GB image pull, WSL2 GPU passthrough, and VLLM_GPU_MEMORY_UTILIZATION=0.85 × VLLM_MAX_MODEL_LEN=18000 against 8 GB VRAM. vllm targets server GPUs (VLLM_GPU_TYPE defaults to "4090"). Likely to OOM.

3. config={"mode": "fast"} — restores the pre-fix behavior deterministically instead of by accident. Still hits the VLM for garbled blocks, so it defers the problem rather than removing it. disable_ocr=True is the only fully server-free path, and only works on PDFs with a clean text layer.

4. Downgrade to marker-pdf==1.10.2 — the config your docs describe, no server dependency. Costs you MarginaliaProcessor, which is why you upgraded.

Worth saying plainly: 2.0.0 turns this repo's marker leg from "pip install and run" into "operate a local inference server." That's a real change in what the harness costs to run.

import os
from typing import Callable, Dict, Optional

import torch
from dotenv import find_dotenv
from pydantic import computed_field
from pydantic_settings import BaseSettings
from pathlib import Path
from platformdirs import user_cache_dir


class Settings(BaseSettings):
    # General
    TORCH_DEVICE: Optional[str] = None
    IMAGE_DPI: int = 96  # used for layout + text detection (coarse structure)
    IMAGE_DPI_HIGHRES: int = 192  # used for recognition + table rec (fine glyphs)
    IN_STREAMLIT: bool = False
    DISABLE_TQDM: bool = False

        "https://github.com/satbyy/go-noto-universal/releases/download/v7.0"
    )

    @computed_field
    def MODEL_DTYPE(self) -> torch.dtype:
        if self.TORCH_DEVICE_MODEL == "cpu":
            return torch.float32
        return torch.float16

    @computed_field
    def MODEL_DTYPE_BFLOAT(self) -> torch.dtype:
        if self.TORCH_DEVICE_MODEL == "cpu":
            return torch.float32
        return torch.bfloat16

    @computed_field
    def INFERENCE_MODE(self) -> Callable:
        return torch.inference_mode

    class Config:
        env_file = find_dotenv("local.env")
        extra = "ignore"

Two things I have not done: no conversion run yet, and no doc updates. On the first real run, expect surya to pull the surya-ocr-2 GGUF + mmproj from HuggingFace before it converts anything.

One likely snag worth naming now: SURYA_INFERENCE_CTX_PER_SLOT is 12288 and total ctx is max(16384, PARALLEL * 12288). On 8 GB VRAM shared with the vision tower, that can overflow and llama.cpp will either spill layers to CPU or fail to start. If it does, SURYA_INFERENCE_PARALLEL=1 is the first knob.

import os
import shutil
from pathlib import Path

os.environ.setdefault("SURYA_INFERENCE_BACKEND", "llamacpp")

if shutil.which("llama-server") is None:
    _llama_server = (
        Path(os.environ.get("LOCALAPPDATA", "")) / "llama.cpp" / "llama-server.exe"
    )
    if _llama_server.is_file():
        os.environ.setdefault("LLAMA_CPP_BINARY", str(_llama_server))

from surya.inference import _autodetect_backend
from surya.inference.backends.llamacpp import _resolve_llama_server_binary

print("on PATH  :", shutil.which("llama-server"))

Verified in exactly your failure condition — llama-server not on PATH, fallback resolves anyway:


on PATH  : None
fallback : C:\Users\LOQ\AppData\Local\llama.cpp\llama-server.exe
backend  : llamacpp
resolved : C:\Users\LOQ\AppData\Local\llama.cpp\llama-server.exe
Diagnosis was: PATH was written correctly to the registry, but your terminal is a child of the IDE, which captured its environment at launch — so even a new tab inherits the stale copy. Restarting the IDE would fix it; the fallback in marker_test.py means you don't have to. Once you do restart at some point, shutil.which succeeds and the fallback silently stops being used.

Re-run as-is and it should get past binary resolution. Two things to expect on this first successful run:

A HuggingFace pull of the surya-ocr-2 GGUF + mmproj before any conversion happens
The KV-cache sizing risk I mentioned — max(16384, PARALLEL * 12288) against 7 GB free VRAM, shared with the vision tower. If llama-server fails to start or crawls, set SURYA_INFERENCE_PARALLEL=1
I'd also stop it after the first PDF rather than letting both 250-page files run, so you can sanity-check the output before spending the full conversion time.

Still not done, and still worth doing once a run succeeds: recording the llamacpp requirement in CLAUDE.md. Right now the file says marker is pip install and run, which is no longer true — a fresh clone on another machine hits the Docker error with nothing explaining it.

    "block": BLOCK_PROMPT,

# JSON schema for LAYOUT_PROMPT — enforced via vllm guided decoding so the
# model can't emit malformed JSON. bbox is a "x0 y0 x1 y1" string (model's
# training-time format); count is a non-negative integer.
LAYOUT_LABEL_SET = [
    "Caption",
    "Footnote",
    "Equation-Block",
    "List-Group",
    "Page-Header",
    "Page-Footer",
    "Image",
    "Section-Header",
    "Table",
    "Text",
    "Complex-Block",
    "Code-Block",
    "Form",
    "Table-Of-Contents",
    "Figure",
    "Chemical-Block",
    "Diagram",
    "Bibliography",
    "Blank-Page",
]

LAYOUT_JSON_SCHEMA = {
    "type": "array",
    "maxItems": 200,
    "items": {
        "type": "object",
        "properties": {
            "label": {"type": "string", "enum": LAYOUT_LABEL_SET},
            "bbox": {
                "type": "string",
                "pattern": r"^\d{1,4} \d{1,4} \d{1,4} \d{1,4}$",
            },
            "count": {"type": "integer", "minimum": 0, "maximum": 10000},
        },
        "required": ["label", "bbox", "count"],
        "additionalProperties": False,
    },
}


# JSON schema for TABLE_REC_PROMPT — array of {label: Row|Col, bbox: "x0 y0 x1 y1"}.
Found it. The schema uses pattern with anchored \d{1,4} quantifiers plus integer minimum/maximum — llama.cpp converts JSON Schema to GBNF internally and chokes on this; vllm (which surya defaults to) handles it natively.

surya already ships the escape hatch, and notably applies it to the sibling setting — SURYA_GUIDED_TABLE_REC is disabled by default with the note "The model produces well-formed JSON without the schema." Same reasoning applies here:

Your existing output was not corrupted. Both .md files in marker_results/ are dated 7/16 — before the upgrade — so they're still the 1.10.2 output. Today's failed runs wrote nothing, because marker_test.py only writes after a successful convert.

Where things stand in the script: backend forced to llamacpp, binary fallback for stale PATH, guided layout off. Each fixed a real failure you hit.

Before you re-run the full corpus, one caution. balanced mode runs the VLM for layout on every page — that's 500 pages through llama.cpp on an 8 GB laptop GPU. This could plausibly run for many hours, and you'd be discovering that after committing to it. page_range exists on the PDF provider (providers/pdf.py:34), so a 5-page smoke test is cheap:


converter = PdfConverter(
    artifact_dict=create_model_dict(),
    config={"page_range": list(range(5))},
)
That confirms the grammar fix end-to-end and gives you a real per-page timing number to extrapolate from. If it's too slow, mode: "fast" uses the local rf-detr layout model and only touches the VLM for garbled blocks — much faster, and it's what you were unknowingly running before the CUDA fix anyway.

Root cause is precise: loss_rnnt.py:22-23 guards the import with is_torchaudio_available(), which checks whether the package is installed — not whether it loads. So the stale torchaudio passes the guard, then dies at the DLL level.

That means uninstalling is the fix, not replacing: the guard flips to False and the import is skipped. There's also no torchaudio==2.13.0 on cu130 to install anyway. Nothing in marker/surya uses it — only transformers' audio models, which marker never touches.

I want to be straight about this one: I told you two messages ago that torchaudio was "inert" and left it in place on that basis. That was wrong, and it cost you a 300-second timeout plus a failed run. The grep I used to justify it had a glob exclusion that made ripgrep match nothing at all, and I read an empty result as "nothing imports this" instead of questioning why a 3 GB dependency tree had zero hits. Had I removed it when I first noticed the version mismatch, this run wouldn't have failed.

The failure was a delayed consequence of the cu130 upgrade: torchaudio 2.11's compiled extension is ABI-bound to torch 2.11, so replacing torch orphaned it. It only surfaced now because ocr_error is the first thing that pulls transformers' loss module.

Also worth noting — this ran in a subprocess server, so the traceback never propagated as an import error; you got an opaque "server failed to become healthy" with the real cause buried in captured logs.

c:\Users\LOQ\Desktop\Projects\Document-Loaders\codes\marker_test.py
SO here's the thing:

I used a 48 page PDF
It slowed down WAY TOO MUCH....not sure if it was stuck too
Whats so different with this marker-pdf than before...skim through this session to tell me

Dont change anything for now
Skimming back through what we established this session — the slowdown is expected, not a bug. Three changes stacked, and I set two of them in motion.

What marker actually does per page now
1.10.2 (what your existing marker_results/ came from): layout was a dedicated discriminative model — roughly one forward pass per page — with OCR only where the text layer was bad. All in-process.

2.0.0 in balanced mode: layout is done by surya-ocr-2, a vision-language model that generates JSON describing the page, plus full-page OCR by the same VLM. That's autoregressive token generation per page instead of a single forward pass. The budgets in settings.py are SURYA_MAX_TOKENS_LAYOUT = 3072 and SURYA_MAX_TOKENS_FULL_PAGE = 12288. Those are ceilings, not typical — but a dense tender-document page still means thousands of generated tokens where 1.10.2 needed none.

Plus it now runs out-of-process. The VLM lives in a separate server behind HTTP.

Why it's especially slow on your box
You're not running the backend upstream designed for. The auto-detect wanted vllm — continuous batching, paged attention, built to overlap many page requests at once. That needs Docker, which is why we fell back to llama.cpp, which processes essentially one request at a time. SURYA_INFERENCE_PARALLEL slots don't buy you much there.

On top of that: quantized GGUF on an 8 GB laptop GPU, ~7 GB free, with a vision tower encoding a full page image per request, and total KV context of max(16384, PARALLEL × 12288). If that overflows VRAM, llama.cpp spills layers to CPU and throughput collapses — which looks exactly like "stuck."

So per page you went from one detector pass to encode an image + generate thousands of tokens, serially, on a laptop GPU. 48 pages of that is plausibly an hour-plus. Nothing is necessarily wrong.

The honest causal chain
Your original question was just about page headers. Restoring CUDA — my call — is what flipped mode from fast to balanced, because pdf.py:130-133 derives it from the device. Ironically, the CPU-torch state you were in before was faster for this workload, because fast mode uses the local rf-detr/onnx layout model and only calls the VLM for garbled blocks.

I also disabled SURYA_GUIDED_LAYOUT to get past the grammar error. That removes the schema constraint on output, so the model is free to generate longer layout responses than it would have. Probably a minor factor next to the above, but it's not zero, and I should flag it as something I changed that affects speed.

Stuck vs. slow
Distinguishable without changing anything — llama.cpp writes to ~/.cache/datalab/surya/llamacpp_server.log (spawn.py:152-154), and it logs per-request timings. That plus nvidia-smi utilization tells you whether it's grinding or hung. Say the word and I'll read them — no edits.

Where this leaves you
Roughly three real options, none of which I'll act on now:

mode: "fast" — closest to your 1.10.2 performance profile, keeps MarginaliaProcessor. Likely the right fit for this hardware.
Stay on balanced — best quality, accept hours per document, ideally overnight.
Docker + vllm — the intended GPU path, but 8 GB VRAM against VLLM_GPU_MEMORY_UTILIZATION 0.85 and VLLM_MAX_MODEL_LEN 18000 makes OOM likely.
Worth naming plainly: 2.0.0 turned marker from a local-model pipeline into a VLM-server pipeline. For a side-by-side comparison harness on a laptop, that's a genuine change in what this leg of the project costs to run — and fast mode is the closest thing to the tool you originally benchmarked.

1. maximum recursion depth exceeded — render-time, not conversion. The line before it comes from markdown.py:182, inside table rendering. Identical output — same SHA256 (B3409FC4…) both runs.

The cache you're thinking of isn't the one that matters. Nothing about the PDF is ever fetched — marker sends no document data anywhere. The only network use is acquiring models, and those were already on disk from the earlier run that downloaded surya-2.gguf (1.27 GB) and surya-2-mmproj.gguf (205 MB).

Both caches are populated:

~/.cache/huggingface/hub/models--datalab-to--surya-ocr-2-gguf — the VLM for llama.cpp
%LOCALAPPDATA%\datalab\datalab\Cache\models\ — layout, ocr_error_detection, table_recognition, text_detection, text_recognition
So a brand-new PDF is irrelevant to whether network is needed. Once those exist, marker is fully offline. Online, huggingface_hub just makes a revision check (that's the "unauthenticated requests" warning) and serves the cached files either way; offline it skips straight to cache.

The identical hashes also tell you something useful for a comparison harness: this pipeline is deterministic — temperature 0, use_llm=False, no external LLM service. Same input, same bytes out.

Caveat worth keeping: this is a 1-page text-layer resume, so it says nothing about the 48-page case. The slowdown there was VLM decoding volume, not downloads.

Answer briefly:
runs in process vs out of process
1.10.2 — in-process: marker imported surya, loaded the layout/OCR models into the same Python process via torch, and called them as function calls. One process, one GPU allocation, tensors passed in memory. Crash = a normal Python traceback in your script.

2.0.0 — out-of-process (VLM only): surya spawns llama-server.exe as a separate OS process, then talks to it over HTTP on localhost using the OpenAI chat-completions protocol. Page images are encoded and sent as requests; layout/OCR come back as generated JSON text.

What that actually changes for you:

Startup cost — the server must load the GGUF and pass a health check (300 s timeout) before any page converts
Failures are opaque — the torchaudio crash was a traceback in the child, so you got "server failed to become healthy" instead of an ImportError; the real cause only appeared in captured logs
Serialization overhead — images out, tokens back, over HTTP, per page
Lifecycle to manage — sentinel file, port, SURYA_INFERENCE_KEEP_ALIVE, and orphaned processes when you Ctrl+C (your atexit interrupt)
Two GPU consumers — torch in your process, llama.cpp in the server, both competing for the same 8 GB
Not everything moved out. The non-VLM models (text_detection, ocr_error_detection, table_recognition, rf-detr layout) still run in-process under torch — which is why torch's CUDA build still matters. It's a hybrid: classical models in-process, VLM out-of-process.

The reason for the split is that vllm/llama.cpp are dedicated inference servers with batching and KV-cache management you can't get from a plain in-process model.generate(). Upstream traded simplicity for throughput — a good trade on a server GPU, less so on your laptop.

fast is one of two conversion pipelines in marker-pdf 2.0.0 (the other is balanced), selected by PdfConverter.mode. It's not a quality knob layered on one pipeline — it swaps out the layout model and the OCR strategy, so the same PDF genuinely extracts differently.

How it gets picked
pdf.py:130-135 — if you don't pass mode, it's inferred from the device:


config["mode"] = "balanced" if settings.TORCH_DEVICE_MODEL == "cuda" else "fast"
That's the trap your CLAUDE.md already flags: a CPU-only torch in .venv-marker silently puts you on fast. marker_test.py:49 passes no config, so your runs are device-dependent today. CLI equivalent is --mode {balanced,fast} (parser.py:85).

What fast actually changes
1. Layout model — fast_layout_model (lightweight rf-detr/onnx) instead of the VLM layout_model (layout.py:87-93). disable_ocr=True also forces this path regardless of mode.

2. Reading order — in fast, the rf-detr reading-order head is skipped on pages that have a text layer; those get ordered from the PDF character stream via pdftext instead, and the head only runs on textless/scanned pages (layout.py:140-150). Marker's own comment claims pdftext order beats the learned head on multi-column pages (75% vs 56% on olmocr-bench). This is directly relevant to the reordering defects in your Marker-PDF Report.md — the two modes use entirely different ordering mechanisms, so those defects may not reproduce across modes.

3. OCR granularity — the core difference. balanced promotes any page containing a flagged (garbled/empty) block to full-page VLM OCR; fast repairs just the individual flagged blocks in place and keeps the rest of the page's pdftext content (line.py:86-96, ocr.py:111-116). Escape hatch: if more than block_ocr_promote_fraction (0.5) of a page's blocks are bad, fast promotes to full-page anyway.

4. Table acceptance threshold — min_recon_score auto-defaults to 0.75 in balanced vs 0.5 in fast (table.py:121-125). fast accepts lower-confidence pdftext table reconstructions rather than paying for a VLM crop-OCR fallback. Given your logged table-corruption defects, this is likely the single biggest quality lever between modes.

5. Inline math — ocr_inline_math auto-resolves to on for balanced only (equation.py:78-81). Display equations are still OCR'd in both modes; inline math in text blocks is left as garbled pdftext glyphs in fast.

6. No inference server — in fast, the VLM manager is lazy, so a clean digital PDF never spawns one at all (models.py:26-31). Worth knowing given your llama.cpp/SURYA_GUIDED_LAYOUT workarounds at the top of marker_test.py — those only bite on paths that actually call the VLM.

Practical read
fast ≈ "trust the PDF text layer; use the VLM only surgically." balanced ≈ "trust the VLM; rebuild whole pages." Neither is strictly better — fast can win on reading order for digital multi-column PDFs, balanced wins on scanned pages, inline math, and low-confidence tables.

If you want your comparison baseline to be reproducible, add config={"mode": "balanced"} (or "fast") to the PdfConverter(...) call at marker_test.py:49. Want me to make that change?

Thats what u told me in a previous session:
mode: "fast" — closest to your 1.10.2 performance profile, keeps MarginaliaProcessor. Likely the right fit for this hardware.

"""RfDetrTorch — rf-detr (Roboflow) detector via the vendored model copy (no rfdetr package).

Backs ``fast_layout``. Inference goes through the slimmed, detection-only model
definition vendored under ``surya.common.rfdetr`` (validated byte-for-byte against the
upstream rfdetr package). Pure PyTorch — runs on cpu/mps/cuda.

Model dir layout (downloaded from the Hub):
  rfdetr_<task>.pth   the fine-tuned rf-detr weights
  config.json         {"arch": "rf-detr-large", "categories": [{"id", "name"}, ...], ...}
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import glob
import json
import os
from typing import List, Optional

from PIL import Image


class _DetList(list):
    """A list of detections that can also carry the page's encoder feature map (.features)."""

    features = None


_MPS_OP_OK: Optional[bool] = None


def _mps_op_supported() -> bool:
    """Probe (once) whether the DINOv2 pos-embed op the rf-detr backbone needs runs on
    MPS. It uses antialiased bicubic (aten::_upsample_bicubic2d_aa), which has no MPS
    kernel — it only works if PYTORCH_ENABLE_MPS_FALLBACK=1 was set before `import torch`
    (surya/__init__.py sets it, but that's too late if torch was already imported).
    Probing at runtime is the only reliable signal, since the env var reads "1" even when
    torch ignored it."""
    global _MPS_OP_OK
    if _MPS_OP_OK is None:
        import warnings

        import torch

        try:
            with warnings.catch_warnings():
                warnings.simplefilter("ignore")
                torch.nn.functional.interpolate(
                    torch.zeros(1, 1, 4, 4, device="mps"),
                    size=(6, 6),
                    mode="bicubic",
                    antialias=True,
                )
            _MPS_OP_OK = True
        except Exception:
            _MPS_OP_OK = False
    return _MPS_OP_OK


def _pick_device(device: Optional[str]) -> str:
    import torch

def _pick_device(device: Optional[str]) -> str:
    import torch

    if device:
        return device
    if torch.cuda.is_available():
        return "cuda"
    # Use MPS only if the CPU-fallback for the unsupported bicubic op is actually live
    # (see _mps_op_supported); otherwise auto-select CPU (rf-detr is ~0.3s/page on CPU)
    # so Apple Silicon never crashes regardless of import order.
    if torch.backends.mps.is_available() and _mps_op_supported():
        return "mps"
    return "cpu"


class RfDetrTorch:
    def __init__(
        self,
        model_dir: str,
        num_threads: Optional[int] = None,
        device: Optional[str] = None,
    ):
        import torch

        if num_threads:
            torch.set_num_threads(int(num_threads))

        self.device = _pick_device(device)
        if self.device == "mps" and not _mps_op_supported():
            # Only reached when the user explicitly forces mps (auto-select already
            # probes and drops to CPU — see _pick_device). The DINOv2 pos-embed op has
            # no MPS kernel and will raise unless PYTORCH_ENABLE_MPS_FALLBACK=1 was set

Change is in (marker_test.py:49-56). The run is in flight — here's what I verified statically while it goes.

Yes, fast still works in 2.0.0 — with two corrections to what I told you before
The MarginaliaProcessor claim holds. It's in default_processors at pdf.py:94, unconditionally — mode never touches the processor list. Header/footer stripping and your MarginaliaProcessor_header_zone tuning work identically in both modes.

But "closest to your 1.10.2 profile" was too strong. I'd said that on the assumption fast is the lighter/older path. Looking at the actual code, fast in 2.0.0 is a new pipeline, not a preserved legacy one — rf-detr layout, pdftext-derived reading order, block-surgical OCR. It's cheaper than balanced, but it is not 1.10.2's pipeline. Your marker_results/ tree still needs a full regeneration to be a valid baseline; pinning the mode doesn't retroactively make old output comparable.

Two things that could have blocked it, and didn't
"onnx" is a docstring lie. Marker's comments repeatedly say rf-detr/onnx (models.py:20, layout.py:29), and there's no onnxruntime in .venv-marker. But the loader is surya/common/rfdetr_torch.py — "Pure PyTorch — runs on cpu/mps/cuda", using a vendored model copy. No extra dependency needed. Worth knowing before you chase a phantom install.

It uses your GPU, not CPU. _pick_device returns "cuda" whenever torch.cuda.is_available() (rfdetr_torch.py:58-70). So fast ≠ "CPU mode" — rf-detr runs on the 5050, and your cu130/sm_120 build still matters. The CUDA setup in CLAUDE.md is not made redundant by this change.

One genuine cost
The checkpoint hf://datalab-to/surya_layout2 is not in your HF cache — I checked, only surya-ocr-2-gguf is there. First fast-mode run downloads it via snapshot_download (rfdetr_torch.py:184-194), so this run needs network and will be slow up front. That's what's happening right now.

Also: llama-server is still reachable-on-demand, not eliminated. Equation OCR stays on in both modes (equation.py:41-46), plus table OCR fallback and garbled-block repair. Your SURYA_INFERENCE_BACKEND/LLAMA_CPP_BINARY shims at the top of the script are still load-bearing. The SURYA_GUIDED_LAYOUT=false workaround becomes inert in fast (it targets the VLM layout model) but is harmless to keep.

I have attached a conversation with an LLM below, consider it, review it, analyze it, and then implement accordingly:

Your current code processes each entire document sequentially in `fast` mode. To process each PDF in parallel 20-page batches, change it to:
* Use `balanced` mode.
* Determine each PDF’s page count.
* Generate ranges such as `0–19`, `20–39`, etc.
* Run a separate `PdfConverter` inside each worker.
* Merge the Markdown results in page order.
* Limit concurrency—start with two workers.
Add this import:
```python
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed
from pypdf import PdfReader
```
Set the Surya options before importing Marker:
```python
os.environ.setdefault("SURYA_INFERENCE_KEEP_ALIVE", "1")
os.environ.setdefault("SURYA_INFERENCE_PARALLEL", "8")
```
Then add:
```python
BATCH_SIZE = 20
MAX_PARALLEL_BATCHES = 2
```
Create a batch-conversion function:
```python
def convert_batch(
    source_path: Path,
    start_page: int,
    end_page: int,
) -> tuple[int, str]:
    # Each worker gets its own converter. Do not share one PdfConverter
    # instance between concurrent workers.
    converter = PdfConverter(
        artifact_dict=create_model_dict(),
        config={
            "mode": "balanced",
            "page_range": list(range(start_page, end_page + 1)),

            # Optional large-header removal settings:
            "keep_pageheader_in_output": False,
            "keep_pagefooter_in_output": False,
            "header_zone": 0.15,
            "max_height_frac": 0.08,
            "max_chars": 300,
        },
    )

    rendered = converter(str(source_path))
    markdown, _, _ = text_from_rendered(rendered)

    return start_page, markdown
```

Replace your existing `converter = PdfConverter(...)` and conversion loop with:

```python
converted = 0
failures: list[tuple[Path, Exception]] = []

for source_path in source_files:
    output_path = output_path_for(source_path)
    output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    print(f"Converting: {source_path.relative_to(PROJECT_ROOT)}")

    try:
        if source_path.suffix.lower() != ".pdf":
            raise ValueError("Parallel page batching currently supports PDF files only.")

        page_count = len(PdfReader(str(source_path)).pages)

        batches = [
            (start, min(start + BATCH_SIZE - 1, page_count - 1))
            for start in range(0, page_count, BATCH_SIZE)
        ]

        print(
            f"Processing {page_count} pages in "
            f"{len(batches)} batch(es), with up to "
            f"{MAX_PARALLEL_BATCHES} running concurrently."
        )

        batch_results: dict[int, str] = {}

        with ThreadPoolExecutor(
            max_workers=MAX_PARALLEL_BATCHES
        ) as executor:
            futures = {
                executor.submit(
                    convert_batch,
                    source_path,
                    start_page,
                    end_page,
                ): (start_page, end_page)
                for start_page, end_page in batches
            }

            for future in as_completed(futures):
                start_page, end_page = futures[future]
                _, markdown = future.result()
                batch_results[start_page] = markdown

                print(
                    f"Completed visible pages "
                    f"{start_page + 1}-{end_page + 1}"
                )

        # Preserve the original page order when combining results.
        markdown_output = "\n\n".join(
            batch_results[start_page].rstrip()
            for start_page, _ in batches
        )

        output_path.write_text(markdown_output + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
        converted += 1

        print(f"Markdown written to: {output_path.relative_to(PROJECT_ROOT)}")

    except Exception as error:
        failures.append((source_path, error))
        print(f"FAILED: {source_path.name}: {error}")
```

Install the page-count dependency if necessary:

```powershell
uv add pypdf
```

Then your existing single command remains:

```powershell
uv run python marker_test.py
```

Important caution: your script forces the `llamacpp` inference backend. Two simultaneous converters may contend for the same inference server or attempt overlapping initialization. Start with:

```python
MAX_PARALLEL_BATCHES = 2
```

If you encounter server startup conflicts, out-of-memory errors, or slower performance, use one document-level worker and let:

```python
SURYA_INFERENCE_PARALLEL=8
```

provide inference-level parallelism instead. Also, parallel 20-page batches do not inherently improve recognition quality; they mainly change scheduling and memory behavior.

import inspect

def strings_to_classes(items: List[str]) -> List[type]:
    classes = []
    for item in items:
        module_name, class_name = item.rsplit(".", 1)
        module = import_module(module_name)
        classes.append(getattr(module, class_name))
    return classes


def classes_to_strings(items: List[type]) -> List[str]:
    for item in items:
        if not inspect.isclass(item):
            raise ValueError(f"Item {item} is not a class")

    return [f"{item.__module__}.{item.__name__}" for item in items]


def verify_config_keys(obj):
    annotations = inspect.get_annotations(obj.__class__)

    none_vals = ""
    for attr_name, annotation in annotations.items():
        if isinstance(annotation, type(Annotated[str, ""])):
            value = getattr(obj, attr_name)
            if value is None:
                none_vals += f"{attr_name}, "

    assert len(none_vals) == 0, (
        f"In order to use {obj.__class__.__name__}, you must set the configuration values `{none_vals}`."
    )


def assign_config(cls, config: BaseModel | dict | None):
    cls_name = cls.__class__.__name__
    if config is None:
        return
    elif isinstance(config, BaseModel):
        dict_config = config.dict()
    elif isinstance(config, dict):
        dict_config = config
    else:
        raise ValueError("config must be a dict or a pydantic BaseModel")

    for k in dict_config:
        if hasattr(cls, k):
            setattr(cls, k, dict_config[k])

from typing import Annotated

from marker.processors import BaseProcessor
from marker.schema import BlockTypes
from marker.schema.document import Document

# Text-carrying leaf types eligible for relabeling. Footnotes are deliberately
# excluded (marker keeps footnotes); pictures/figures/tables are never
# marginalia.
_ELIGIBLE = (BlockTypes.Text, BlockTypes.SectionHeader, BlockTypes.Caption)


class MarginaliaProcessor(BaseProcessor):
    """Relabel running headers/footers the layout model missed.

    A block is marginalia only when ALL of these hold - each guard exists to
    protect a real-content case:
      - it sits entirely inside the top/bottom margin zone (body text is out)
      - it is the extremal text block on the page (nothing above a header /
        below a footer - protects edge-adjacent body paragraphs)
      - it is running-head sized (small type; protects title pages, where
        titles are near the top but large)
      - it is short (a line or two; protects tall edge columns)
    """

    block_types = _ELIGIBLE
    header_zone: Annotated[
        float, "Top fraction of the page that can hold a running header."
    ] = 0.08
    footer_zone: Annotated[
        float, "Bottom fraction of the page that can hold a running footer."
    ] = 0.13
    max_height_frac: Annotated[
        float,
        "Maximum block height (fraction of page height) - running heads are",
        "one or two small lines.",
    ] = 0.035
    max_chars: Annotated[int, "Maximum text length for a marginalia block."] = 150
    # Mirror the renderer flags: a user who asked to keep headers/footers in
    # the output shouldn't have positionally-detected ones suppressed here.
    keep_pageheader_in_output: Annotated[
        bool, "Keep page headers in the final output."
    ] = False
    keep_pagefooter_in_output: Annotated[
        bool, "Keep page footers in the final output."
    ] = False

    def __call__(self, document: Document):
        for page in document.pages:
            text_blocks = [
                b
                for b in page.structure_blocks(document)
                if b.block_type
                in (*_ELIGIBLE, BlockTypes.ListGroup, BlockTypes.TextInlineMath)
                and not getattr(b, "ignore_for_output", False)

from surya.fast_layout import FastLayoutPredictor
from surya.inference import SuryaInferenceManager
from surya.layout import LayoutPredictor
from surya.ocr_error import OCRErrorPredictor
from surya.recognition import RecognitionPredictor


def create_model_dict(
    device=None,
    dtype=None,
    attention_implementation: str | None = None,
    inference_manager: SuryaInferenceManager | None = None,
    inference_backend: str | None = None,
) -> dict:
    """Build the predictor set marker uses.

    Two converter modes share this set:
      - balanced (default): the VLM ``layout_model`` + full-page
        ``recognition_model``.
      - fast: the lightweight rf-detr ``fast_layout_model`` + block-mode OCR,
        OCRing only garbled/empty content.

    All the heavy models run in shared surya servers, so every predictor here is
    a thin client and marker worker processes stay light (this is what lets many
    workers share one GPU without each loading a model):
      - ``layout_model`` / ``recognition_model``: clients of the VLM
        ``inference_manager`` (lazy - only spawns a server when OCR is actually
        needed, so clean digital docs in fast mode never start it).
      - ``fast_layout_model``: a client of the shared fast-layout server (owns
        the single rf-detr instance + continuous-batches across all clients).
        Holds no model; spawns/attaches the server on first call, not here.
      - ``ocr_error_model``: a client of the shared ocr-error server (the
        DistilBert model runs in one server process; workers POST text to it).

    Every predictor here is now a thin server client, so marker worker
    processes hold NO models and many can share one GPU without each loading a
    copy. Tables are reconstructed from the PDF text layer (pdftext heuristics)
    for digital pages and from full-page OCR for scanned pages - there is no
    dedicated table model. ``device``/``dtype``/``attention_implementation`` are
    accepted for call-site compatibility (e.g. worker_init) but are now no-ops -
    model devices are set server-side.
    """
    manager = inference_manager or SuryaInferenceManager(method=inference_backend)
    return {
        "inference_manager": manager,
        "layout_model": LayoutPredictor(manager),
        # Thin client of the shared fast-layout server (holds no model; the
        # server owns the one rf-detr instance and continuous-batches across
        # workers). Cheap to construct even in balanced mode, where it is never
        # called. The reading-order head defaults off: pdftext pages are
        # reordered from the PDF's character order (LineBuilder), so learned
        # order is requested per call only for the pages that need it.
        "fast_layout_model": FastLayoutPredictor(use_order=False),
        "recognition_model": RecognitionPredictor(manager),
        # Thin client of the shared ocr-error server (the DistilBert model runs
        # in one server process; N marker workers just POST text to it). Holds
        # no model, so it doesn't add per-worker GPU load. device/dtype/
        # attention_implementation are server-side now and ignored here (kept in
        # the signature for call-site compatibility, e.g. worker_init).
        "ocr_error_model": OCRErrorPredictor(),
    }


def shutdown_models(model_dict: dict) -> None:
    """Stop the shared inference server if this process spawned it."""
    manager = model_dict.get("inference_manager")
    if manager is not None:
        manager.stop()

class PdfConverter(BaseConverter):
    """
    A converter for processing and rendering PDF files into Markdown, JSON, HTML and other formats.
    """

        Dict[BlockTypes, Type[Block]],
        "A mapping to override the default block classes for specific block types.",
        "The keys are `BlockTypes` enum values, representing the types of blocks,",
        "and the values are corresponding `Block` class implementations to use",
        "instead of the defaults.",
    ] = defaultdict()
    use_llm: Annotated[
        bool,
        "Enable higher quality processing with LLMs.",
    ] = False
    mode: Annotated[
        str,
        "Conversion mode. 'balanced' uses the VLM layout model and full-page OCR;",
        "'fast' uses lightweight rf-detr/onnx detectors for layout + tables and only",
        "block-OCRs garbled/empty content. Defaults by device: 'balanced' on GPU,",
        "'fast' on CPU/MPS.",
    ] = None
    default_processors: Tuple[BaseProcessor, ...] = (
        BlockRelabelProcessor,
        LineMergeProcessor,
        BlockquoteProcessor,
        CodeProcessor,
        DocumentTOCProcessor,
        EquationProcessor,
        FootnoteProcessor,
        IgnoreTextProcessor,

        # Total KV-cache budget. llama-server divides --ctx-size across
        # --parallel slots, so a too-small total silently truncates outputs
        # once each slot's share fills. Scale with parallel by default;
        # SURYA_INFERENCE_CTX_SIZE overrides to a fixed value if set.
        parallel = settings.SURYA_INFERENCE_PARALLEL or self.DEFAULT_PARALLEL
        per_slot = settings.SURYA_INFERENCE_CTX_PER_SLOT
        ctx_size = settings.SURYA_INFERENCE_CTX_SIZE
        if ctx_size is None:
            ctx_size = max(16384, parallel * per_slot)
        effective_per_slot = ctx_size // max(parallel, 1)
        logger.info(
            f"llama-server ctx-size={ctx_size} "
            f"(~{effective_per_slot}/slot Ã— {parallel} parallel slots)"
        )
        if effective_per_slot < per_slot:
            logger.warning(
                f"per-slot ctx ({effective_per_slot}) is below recommended "
                f"{per_slot}; outputs may truncate. Raise "
                f"SURYA_INFERENCE_CTX_SIZE or SURYA_INFERENCE_CTX_PER_SLOT, "

class SuryaInferenceManager:
    """Single entry point for VLM inference. Construct once per process."""

    def __init__(self, method: Optional[str] = None, lazy: bool = True):
        self.method = method or _autodetect_backend()
        self.backend: Backend = _build_backend(self.method)
        if not lazy:
            self.backend.start()

    def start(self) -> None:
        self.backend.start()

    def stop(self) -> None:
        self.backend.stop()

    def generate(self, batch: List[BatchInputItem]) -> List[BatchOutputItem]:
        return self.backend.generate(batch)

    def capacity(self) -> int:
        """Server concurrency capacity (see Backend.capacity)."""
        return self.backend.capacity()


# Module-level lazy singleton for callers that don't want explicit construction
# (notebooks, ad-hoc scripts). Surya's own models.py and marker should use
# explicit construction.
_default_manager: Optional[SuryaInferenceManager] = None

"""Smoke test: two concurrent 3-page batches sharing one model dict."""

import sys
import time
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed
from pathlib import Path

REPO = Path(r"c:\Users\LOQ\Desktop\Projects\Document-Loaders")
sys.path.insert(0, str(REPO / "codes"))

import marker_test as m
from marker.models import create_model_dict, shutdown_models

pdf = sorted((REPO / "source").rglob("*.pdf"))[0]
batches = [(0, 2), (3, 5)]

t0 = time.time()
model_dict = create_model_dict()
model_dict["inference_manager"].start()
print(f"[{time.time() - t0:6.1f}s] models up, server started", flush=True)

results = {}
try:
    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as ex:
        futures = {
            ex.submit(m.convert_batch, model_dict, pdf, s, e): (s, e)
            for s, e in batches
        }
        for f in as_completed(futures):
            s, e = futures[f]
            _, md = f.result()
            results[s] = md
            print(
                f"[{time.time() - t0:6.1f}s] batch pages {s + 1}-{e + 1}: "
                f"{len(md)} chars",
                flush=True,
            )
finally:
    shutdown_models(model_dict)

merged = "\n\n".join(results[s].rstrip() for s, _ in batches)
print(f"[{time.time() - t0:6.1f}s] merged: {len(merged)} chars")
print("order preserved:", list(results.keys()) != [] and merged.startswith(results[0][:40].rstrip()[:20]))
print("---- first 400 chars ----")
print(merged[:400])

"""Isolate the llama-server crash: is it concurrency, or the content itself?

Runs the SAME 20-page batches over the first 60 pages, with worker count taken
from argv. Compare `isolate.py 1` (serial) against `isolate.py 2` (concurrent).
"""

import sys
import time
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed
from pathlib import Path

REPO = Path(r"c:\Users\LOQ\Desktop\Projects\Document-Loaders")
sys.path.insert(0, str(REPO / "codes"))

import marker_test as m
from marker.models import create_model_dict

workers = int(sys.argv[1])
pdf = sorted((REPO / "source").rglob("*.pdf"))[0]
batches = [(0, 19), (20, 39), (40, 59)]

t0 = time.time()
model_dict = create_model_dict()
model_dict["inference_manager"].start()
print(f"[{time.time() - t0:6.1f}s] server up | workers={workers}", flush=True)

ok, bad = [], []
with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=workers) as ex:
    futures = {
        ex.submit(m.convert_batch, model_dict, pdf, s, e): (s, e) for s, e in batches
    }
    for f in as_completed(futures):
        s, e = futures[f]
        try:
            _, md = f.result()
            ok.append((s, e))
            print(f"[{time.time() - t0:6.1f}s] OK  pages {s}-{e}: {len(md)} chars", flush=True)
        except Exception as err:
            bad.append((s, e, err))
            print(f"[{time.time() - t0:6.1f}s] ERR pages {s}-{e}: {type(err).__name__}: {err}", flush=True)

print(f"[{time.time() - t0:6.1f}s] DONE workers={workers} ok={len(ok)} err={len(bad)}", flush=True)

Write C:\Users\LOQ\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\c--Users-LOQ-Desktop-Projects-Document-Loaders\ef6b1437-8689-40a4-8abe-becfd6d76142\scratchpad\isolate.py
62 lines
"""Isolate the llama-server crash: is it concurrency, or the content itself?

Runs the SAME 20-page batches over the first 60 pages, with worker count taken
from argv. Compare `isolate.py 1` (serial) against `isolate.py 2` (concurrent).
Needs the __main__ guard: marker spawns child processes on Windows.
"""

import sys
import time
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed
from pathlib import Path

REPO = Path(r"c:\Users\LOQ\Desktop\Projects\Document-Loaders")
sys.path.insert(0, str(REPO / "codes"))

import marker_test as m
from marker.models import create_model_dict


def run() -> None:
    workers = int(sys.argv[1])
    pdf = sorted((REPO / "source").rglob("*.pdf"))[0]
    batches = [(0, 19), (20, 39), (40, 59)]

    t0 = time.time()
    model_dict = create_model_dict()
    model_dict["inference_manager"].start()
    print(f"[{time.time() - t0:6.1f}s] server up | workers={workers}", flush=True)

    ok, bad = [], []
    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=workers) as ex:
        futures = {
            ex.submit(m.convert_batch, model_dict, pdf, s, e): (s, e)
            for s, e in batches
        }
        for f in as_completed(futures):
            s, e = futures[f]
            try:
                _, md = f.result()
                ok.append((s, e))
                print(
                    f"[{time.time() - t0:6.1f}s] OK  pages {s}-{e}: {len(md)} chars",
                    flush=True,
                )
            except Exception as err:
                bad.append((s, e, err))
                print(
                    f"[{time.time() - t0:6.1f}s] ERR pages {s}-{e}: "
                    f"{type(err).__name__}: {err}",
                    flush=True,
                )

    print(
        f"[{time.time() - t0:6.1f}s] DONE workers={workers} "
        f"ok={len(ok)} err={len(bad)}",
        flush=True,
    )


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run()
